Um, no, not really. I don't get headaches or anything like that. And I haven't been sick. Actually, the last time I was really sick was when I caught that bad bug going around the restaurant, but that was like two years ago. The prep cook made me this massive batch of soup, but he used those tiny little star-shaped noodles instead of the regular long ones. Pastina, I think they call it? It's Italian. I've always wanted to go to Italy, honestly. My friend Sarah went to Rome for her honeymoon and she said the architecture is just unreal. Like, the Colosseum is just sitting right there in the middle of regular city traffic. Can you imagine just driving your car to work past a building that's thousands of years old? The exhaust fumes from all those cars must be so bad for the ancient stone. It makes you wonder how they preserve it all. \n\nBut, um... no, my vision is fine, too. I don't wear glasses. The lighting in the dining room does make everything look a little yellow sometimes, but my eyes are fine. Wait, what was the other thing you asked? Something about shaking?\n\nDoctor: Over what period of time have you noticed these issues with your memory and your ability to focus at work?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's been... I'd say this whole change in me has been building for about a year and two months. Yeah, it started right around the time they asked me to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities. Which was supposed to be this big exciting thing, you know? They gave me a new nametag and everything. It was one of those magnetic ones, not the kind with the pin that ruins your shirts. I ruined so many good blouses with those pins. Especially this one silk shirt I got at a thrift store. It had these tiny little embroidered bees on the collar. Have you ever noticed how bees always seem to know exactly where they're going? Like, they have this built-in GPS for flowers. I read somewhere that they communicate by dancing. Like a little waggle dance to tell the other bees where the good pollen is. It's so fascinating how nature works like that. Just tiny little insects organizing a whole society. \n\nBut, um, yeah. About a year and two months since I started the shift lead stuff. That's when I really started noticing the focus issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your confusion and 'zoning out' happen at specific times of the day, or do you have days where you feel perfectly clear-headed followed by days where you are very confused?\n\nPatient: Um, specific times? I don't... I don't really think so. It's just kind of all the time now. I don't really have those perfectly clear days you're talking about. It's just a constant fog, I guess. It reminds me of this time we drove up to the mountains, my husband and I, for our anniversary. We rented this little cabin, and the fog rolled in so thick you couldn't even see the trees right outside the window. It was kind of spooky, honestly. But they had this amazing fireplace, one of those big stone ones that goes all the way up to the ceiling. We spent hours just watching the fire. Fire is so mesmerizing, isn't it? The way the colors shift from orange to blue at the base. I learned once that the blue part is actually the hottest part of the flame, even though you'd think it would be the bright red part. It's all about the oxygen levels and combustion, I think. Science is just... it's wild how things work. \n\nBut, wait... what did you ask me? Something about the time of day?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if your muscles ever feel stiff, or if you have any trouble with your balance or walking?\n\nPatient: Stiff muscles? Um, no, I don't think so. My balance is fine, too. I mean, I'm on my feet all day carrying heavy trays, so I'd definitely notice if I couldn't walk right or if I was tripping over myself. We have to wear these special non-slip shoes for work, though, to keep from falling. They are honestly so ugly. They look like little black rubber boats. I bought my last pair at this uniform shop right next to the old mall. \n\nThat mall used to have this massive fountain right in the center court where people would throw pennies in and make wishes. I always wondered what happens to all those coins. Like, does someone have to wade in there in rubber boots at the end of the month and scoop them all out with a net? Imagine having that job, just collecting thousands of wet pennies. Pennies smell so strange when they're wet, you know? Kind of like copper and... well, blood, almost. It's that sharp metallic smell. I read once that the smell isn't actually the metal itself, it's a chemical reaction with the natural oils on human skin when you touch it. It's weird how our bodies just react to things without us even knowing. \n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Something about my legs?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a doctor that you have high blood pressure, diabetes, or any problems with your heart?\n\nPatient: High blood pressure or diabetes? Um, no. No doctor's ever told me I had anything like that. My heart's fine too, as far as I know. I did go to the doctor once because my heart was racing, but that was just because I drank way too much espresso. We got this new espresso machine at the restaurant, this huge shiny Italian thing with all these levers and dials. It looks like it belongs on a spaceship or something. The guy who came to install it was telling me how the pressure has to be exactly nine bars to pull a perfect shot. Nine bars! That's like... I don't even know how much pressure that is, but it sounds like a lot. He said if it's too high, the water channels through the coffee puck too fast and it tastes sour. It's crazy how much science goes into just a tiny cup of coffee. \n\nI actually tried to make latte art once, you know, pouring the milk to make a little heart or a leaf? It is so much harder than it looks on those videos online. My leaf ended up looking like a squashed onion. Onions always make me cry so much when I chop them for the prep cooks. They say if you chew gum while you chop onions it stops the crying, but I tried peppermint gum and it just made my eyes burn with mint flavor instead. \n\nWait, sorry. Did you ask me about my heart? Yeah, no, my heart is fine. Just the espresso that one time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your personality, or have you been told that you are acting differently in social situations lately?\n\nPatient: Changes in my personality? Um, well, I guess... I mean, when my husband tries to talk with me lately, I mostly just sit there. It feels like I just can’t get myself to care or start anything, you know? I don’t even really do the things I used to like. I don't really bake, or garden, or go for beach walks much anymore. \n\nWe used to go to the beach all the time to look for sea glass. Have you ever looked for sea glass? It’s actually just old broken bottles that the ocean tumbles around in the sand until the edges are all smooth and frosted. It’s kind of amazing how the water just takes our garbage and turns it into something pretty. I had this big mason jar full of the blue pieces. Blue is the hardest to find because nobody really makes blue glass bottles anymore. It’s mostly just green and brown. Brown glass always reminds me of root beer. My grandpa used to brew his own root beer in his basement when I was little, and the whole house would smell like yeast and sassafras. He had these huge glass jugs with rubber stoppers that would bubble. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I don't really do any of my hobbies anymore. I don't even write in my journal much. Journals are so weird when you think about it. You just write down all your private thoughts in a book that literally anyone could pick up and read. They have those little metal locks on them, but honestly, you can pop those open with a paperclip in two seconds. I used a safety pin to pick the lock on my older sister's diary when I was ten. She was so mad she threw a plastic hairbrush at my head. It had those little plastic balls on the bristles and it actually really hurt. \n\nBut, um... sorry, what was the question? Did you ask about my husband?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any weakness or loss of sensation on just one side of your body?\n\nPatient: Weakness on one side? Um, no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Both sides feel the same to me. I mean, my right arm gets a little tired sometimes from carrying those big oval trays at the restaurant, but that's just normal tired, not weak. Those trays can get so heavy, especially when someone orders the seafood platter. It comes on this massive cast-iron skillet thing that must weigh ten pounds on its own. \n\nCast iron is so heavy. My mom has this cast-iron skillet that belonged to my great-grandmother, and she refuses to wash it with soap. She says it ruins the \"seasoning.\" It's just a layer of baked-on grease, really, but people get so defensive about it. I read that you're actually supposed to rub it with coarse salt and half a potato to clean it. Potatoes are so versatile. You can bake them, fry them, mash them... and apparently use them as a sponge. Did you know they used to use potatoes to make vodka? I guess they still do, but mostly it's grains now. We have this one vodka at the bar that comes in a bottle shaped like a skull. It looks really cool on the shelf when the lights hit it. \n\nThe lights at the restaurant are on these dimmer switches, and the manager is always adjusting them. He says it creates \"ambiance,\" but honestly, by 8 PM it's so dark you can barely read the menus. The menus have this really tiny font, too. It's like they don't want you to know what you're ordering. \n\nBut wait, what were you asking? Something about my arm being tired?\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me what you had for breakfast this morning?\n\nPatient: Breakfast? Um, I didn't really have anything. I was going to have a bowl of cereal, but the milk smelled kind of funny. It wasn't expired yet, but you know when milk just has that... off smell? It comes in those plastic jugs, and I read somewhere online that the plastic can actually leach into the milk if it gets too warm on the delivery truck. Plastic is in literally everything now. They call them microplastics. They even found them at the very bottom of the ocean, which is just so sad. The ocean is supposed to be this big, deep, untouched place, but there are grocery bags just floating around down there looking like jellyfish. \n\nJellyfish are completely fascinating, though. They don't even have brains or a heart. They just float around on the currents stinging things. I got stung by a jellyfish when I was seven on a family trip to Florida. It wrapped right around my ankle. My uncle told me someone had to pee on it to make the stinging stop, but my mom yelled at him and made the lifeguard put vinegar on it instead. Vinegar smells so strong. We use white vinegar to clean the coffee carafes at the restaurant because it gets the hard water stains out better than soap. The water here is really hard, it leaves this white crust on all the sinks if you don't scrub them every single night. \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Did you ask me about cleaning the restaurant?\n\nDoctor: Could you try to count backward from 20 by subtracting 3 each time?\n\nPatient: Count backward? Um, okay. Twenty... then seventeen... and then fourteen... and then eleven... \n\nEleven is a weird number. It's like, the only number that looks like two little sticks standing next to each other. I used to have this pair of walking sticks when I was in Girl Scouts. We went on this huge hiking trip up in the mountains, and I carved my initials into one of them with a pocket knife. Pocket knives are so handy, but you have to be really careful. My brother sliced his thumb open trying to whittle a piece of pine wood once. He bled all over the porch, and my dad had to take him to the emergency room for stitches. \n\nEmergency rooms are always freezing cold. I think they keep it cold on purpose so the germs don't spread or something. I had to go to the ER last year when I slipped on a wet floor at the restaurant. I didn't break anything, but I bruised my tailbone so badly I couldn't sit right for a week. They make you wait in those uncomfortable plastic chairs forever. They have those little TVs in the corner playing daytime talk shows with the volume turned all the way down, but the captions are always slightly delayed, so it's really confusing to watch. \n\nWait, what was I doing? Was I counting? Where did I stop?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that weren't actually there, like people, animals, or objects, even when you know they aren't real?\n\nPatient: Seeing things? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, sometimes out of the corner of my eye I think I see a customer waving me down, but it's just a coat hanging on a chair. People leave their coats on chairs all the time, even when we have a coat rack right by the front door. The coat rack is this really nice dark wood, almost like mahogany, but one of the pegs is loose. I keep meaning to bring a screwdriver from home to fix it, but I always forget. \n\nI'm terrible at remembering tools. My dad had this huge red toolbox in the garage when I was growing up, and every drawer had a different type of wrench or screwdriver. He was obsessed with organizing it. He even traced the outline of every tool onto this foam stuff so you'd know exactly where it went back. Foam is so weird if you think about it. It's mostly just trapped air. They had this massive vat of caramel just spinning around...\n\nWait, sorry, what were you asking me? Something about coats?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a doctor that you have any thyroid problems, vitamin deficiencies, or any other metabolic conditions?\n\nPatient: Thyroid problems? Um, no, I don't think any doctor has ever told me that. I mean, I try to take vitamins sometimes, like those gummy ones? They taste just like candy, which is probably dangerous for kids. I remember my little cousin ate a whole bottle of Flintstones vitamins once because he thought they were sweet tarts. They had to call poison control and everything. Was it about my work schedule?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty in completing familiar tasks at home, such as cooking a meal or using household appliances?\n\nPatient: Familiar tasks? Um, no, I don't think so. I can still cook and everything just fine. I made spaghetti just last night. Boiling water is pretty easy, though my stove takes absolutely forever to heat up. It's one of those electric ones with the flat glass top. I really miss having a gas stove. My old apartment had a gas stove, and it cooked everything so evenly, but I was always terrified I'd leave it on and blow the place up. You know how they add that rotten egg smell to gas so you can tell if there's a leak? Car insurance is such a scam, honestly. You pay them every single month, and then when something actually happens, they fight you on paying for it. Was it about my car?\n\nDoctor: Have you always been someone who prefers to keep to yourself, or have you often felt that people around you, like your coworkers or neighbors, are talking about you or looking at you differently?\n\nPatient: People looking at me? Like, they're watching exactly how I ring things into the POS system. Was it about my husband?\n\nDoctor: When you think these coworkers are watching you and leaving marks, do you feel like you can hear them talking about you when they aren't actually nearby?\n\nPatient: Hearing them? Was it about the register?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these experiences have been going on for over a year; have you had these feelings of being watched or hearing that voice for at least the last six months straight?\n\nPatient: Six months? They buy the absolute cheapest napkins, too—the ones that basically dissolve if you try to wipe up a single drop of condensation. \n\nWater rings on wooden tables drive me crazy, though. Mint is supposed to be refreshing, but I think it just makes cold water hurt your teeth even more. Water is so important to drink, though. Tomato sauce is impossible to get out of white clothes. I ruined my favorite white blouse that way. It was silk, too. Silk is so high maintenance, you always have to dry clean it. The lighting in the dining room does make everything look a little yellow sometimes, but my eyes are fine. Wait, what was the other thing you asked? Something about shaking?\n\nDoctor: Over what period of time have you noticed these issues with your memory and your ability to focus at work?\n\nPatient: Um, well, it's been... I'd say this whole change in me has been building for about a year and two months. Yeah, it started right around the time they asked me to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities. Which was supposed to be this big exciting thing, you know? They gave me a new nametag and everything. It was one of those magnetic ones, not the kind with the pin that ruins your shirts. I ruined so many good blouses with those pins. Especially this one silk shirt I got at a thrift store. It had these tiny little embroidered bees on the collar. Have you ever noticed how bees always seem to know exactly where they're going? Like, they have this built-in GPS for flowers. I read somewhere that they communicate by dancing. Like a little waggle dance to tell the other bees where the good pollen is. It's so fascinating how nature works like that. Just tiny little insects organizing a whole society. \n\nBut, um, yeah. About a year and two months since I started the shift lead stuff. That's when I really started noticing the focus issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that your confusion and 'zoning out' happen at specific times of the day, or do you have days where you feel perfectly clear-headed followed by days where you are very confused?\n\nPatient: Um, specific times? I don't... I don't really think so. It's just kind of all the time now. I don't really have those perfectly clear days you're talking about. It's just a constant fog, I guess. It reminds me of this time we drove up to the mountains, my husband and I, for our anniversary. We rented this little cabin, and the fog rolled in so thick you couldn't even see the trees right outside the window. It was kind of spooky, honestly. But they had this amazing fireplace, one of those big stone ones that goes all the way up to the ceiling. We spent hours just watching the fire. Fire is so mesmerizing, isn't it? The way the colors shift from orange to blue at the base. I learned once that the blue part is actually the hottest part of the flame, even though you'd think it would be the bright red part. It's all about the oxygen levels and combustion, I think. Science is just... it's wild how things work. \n\nBut, wait... what did you ask me? Something about the time of day?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed if your muscles ever feel stiff, or if you have any trouble with your balance or walking?\n\nPatient: Stiff muscles? Um, no, I don't think so. My balance is fine, too. I mean, I'm on my feet all day carrying heavy trays, so I'd definitely notice if I couldn't walk right or if I was tripping over myself. We have to wear these special non-slip shoes for work, though, to keep from falling. They are honestly so ugly. They look like little black rubber boats. I bought my last pair at this uniform shop right next to the old mall. \n\nThat mall used to have this massive fountain right in the center court where people would throw pennies in and make wishes. I always wondered what happens to all those coins. Like, does someone have to wade in there in rubber boots at the end of the month and scoop them all out with a net? Imagine having that job, just collecting thousands of wet pennies. Pennies smell so strange when they're wet, you know? Kind of like copper and... well, blood, almost. It's that sharp metallic smell. I read once that the smell isn't actually the metal itself, it's a chemical reaction with the natural oils on human skin when you touch it. It's weird how our bodies just react to things without us even knowing. \n\nBut, um... wait, what were you asking? Something about my legs?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a doctor that you have high blood pressure, diabetes, or any problems with your heart?\n\nPatient: High blood pressure or diabetes? Um, no. No doctor's ever told me I had anything like that. My heart's fine too, as far as I know. I did go to the doctor once because my heart was racing, but that was just because I drank way too much espresso. We got this new espresso machine at the restaurant, this huge shiny Italian thing with all these levers and dials. It looks like it belongs on a spaceship or something. The guy who came to install it was telling me how the pressure has to be exactly nine bars to pull a perfect shot. Nine bars! That's like... I don't even know how much pressure that is, but it sounds like a lot. He said if it's too high, the water channels through the coffee puck too fast and it tastes sour. It's crazy how much science goes into just a tiny cup of coffee. \n\nI actually tried to make latte art once, you know, pouring the milk to make a little heart or a leaf? It is so much harder than it looks on those videos online. My leaf ended up looking like a squashed onion. Onions always make me cry so much when I chop them for the prep cooks. They say if you chew gum while you chop onions it stops the crying, but I tried peppermint gum and it just made my eyes burn with mint flavor instead. \n\nWait, sorry. Did you ask me about my heart? Yeah, no, my heart is fine. Just the espresso that one time.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any changes in your personality, or have you been told that you are acting differently in social situations lately?\n\nPatient: Changes in my personality? Um, well, I guess... I mean, when my husband tries to talk with me lately, I mostly just sit there. It feels like I just can’t get myself to care or start anything, you know? I don’t even really do the things I used to like. I don't really bake, or garden, or go for beach walks much anymore. \n\nWe used to go to the beach all the time to look for sea glass. Have you ever looked for sea glass? It’s actually just old broken bottles that the ocean tumbles around in the sand until the edges are all smooth and frosted. It’s kind of amazing how the water just takes our garbage and turns it into something pretty. I had this big mason jar full of the blue pieces. Blue is the hardest to find because nobody really makes blue glass bottles anymore. It’s mostly just green and brown. Brown glass always reminds me of root beer. My grandpa used to brew his own root beer in his basement when I was little, and the whole house would smell like yeast and sassafras. He had these huge glass jugs with rubber stoppers that would bubble. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, I don't really do any of my hobbies anymore. I don't even write in my journal much. Journals are so weird when you think about it. You just write down all your private thoughts in a book that literally anyone could pick up and read. They have those little metal locks on them, but honestly, you can pop those open with a paperclip in two seconds. I used a safety pin to pick the lock on my older sister's diary when I was ten. She was so mad she threw a plastic hairbrush at my head. It had those little plastic balls on the bristles and it actually really hurt. \n\nBut, um... sorry, what was the question? Did you ask about my husband?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any weakness or loss of sensation on just one side of your body?\n\nPatient: Weakness on one side? Um, no, I haven't noticed anything like that. Both sides feel the same to me. I mean, my right arm gets a little tired sometimes from carrying those big oval trays at the restaurant, but that's just normal tired, not weak. Those trays can get so heavy, especially when someone orders the seafood platter. It comes on this massive cast-iron skillet thing that must weigh ten pounds on its own. \n\nCast iron is so heavy. My mom has this cast-iron skillet that belonged to my great-grandmother, and she refuses to wash it with soap. She says it ruins the \"seasoning.\" It's just a layer of baked-on grease, really, but people get so defensive about it. I read that you're actually supposed to rub it with coarse salt and half a potato to clean it. Potatoes are so versatile. You can bake them, fry them, mash them... and apparently use them as a sponge. Did you know they used to use potatoes to make vodka? I guess they still do, but mostly it's grains now. We have this one vodka at the bar that comes in a bottle shaped like a skull. It looks really cool on the shelf when the lights hit it. \n\nThe lights at the restaurant are on these dimmer switches, and the manager is always adjusting them. He says it creates \"ambiance,\" but honestly, by 8 PM it's so dark you can barely read the menus. The menus have this really tiny font, too. It's like they don't want you to know what you're ordering. \n\nBut wait, what were you asking? Something about my arm being tired?\n\nDoctor: Could you tell me what you had for breakfast this morning?\n\nPatient: Breakfast? Um, I didn't really have anything. I was going to have a bowl of cereal, but the milk smelled kind of funny. It wasn't expired yet, but you know when milk just has that... off smell? It comes in those plastic jugs, and I read somewhere online that the plastic can actually leach into the milk if it gets too warm on the delivery truck. Plastic is in literally everything now. They call them microplastics. They even found them at the very bottom of the ocean, which is just so sad. The ocean is supposed to be this big, deep, untouched place, but there are grocery bags just floating around down there looking like jellyfish. \n\nJellyfish are completely fascinating, though. They don't even have brains or a heart. They just float around on the currents stinging things. I got stung by a jellyfish when I was seven on a family trip to Florida. It wrapped right around my ankle. My uncle told me someone had to pee on it to make the stinging stop, but my mom yelled at him and made the lifeguard put vinegar on it instead. Vinegar smells so strong. We use white vinegar to clean the coffee carafes at the restaurant because it gets the hard water stains out better than soap. The water here is really hard, it leaves this white crust on all the sinks if you don't scrub them every single night. \n\nWait, sorry, what were we talking about? Did you ask me about cleaning the restaurant?\n\nDoctor: Could you try to count backward from 20 by subtracting 3 each time?\n\nPatient: Count backward? Um, okay. Twenty... then seventeen... and then fourteen... and then eleven... \n\nEleven is a weird number. It's like, the only number that looks like two little sticks standing next to each other. I used to have this pair of walking sticks when I was in Girl Scouts. We went on this huge hiking trip up in the mountains, and I carved my initials into one of them with a pocket knife. Pocket knives are so handy, but you have to be really careful. My brother sliced his thumb open trying to whittle a piece of pine wood once. He bled all over the porch, and my dad had to take him to the emergency room for stitches. \n\nEmergency rooms are always freezing cold. I think they keep it cold on purpose so the germs don't spread or something. I had to go to the ER last year when I slipped on a wet floor at the restaurant. I didn't break anything, but I bruised my tailbone so badly I couldn't sit right for a week. They make you wait in those uncomfortable plastic chairs forever. They have those little TVs in the corner playing daytime talk shows with the volume turned all the way down, but the captions are always slightly delayed, so it's really confusing to watch. \n\nWait, what was I doing? Was I counting? Where did I stop?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever seen things that weren't actually there, like people, animals, or objects, even when you know they aren't real?\n\nPatient: Seeing things? Um, no, I don't think so. I mean, sometimes out of the corner of my eye I think I see a customer waving me down, but it's just a coat hanging on a chair. People leave their coats on chairs all the time, even when we have a coat rack right by the front door. The coat rack is this really nice dark wood, almost like mahogany, but one of the pegs is loose. I keep meaning to bring a screwdriver from home to fix it, but I always forget. \n\nI'm terrible at remembering tools. My dad had this huge red toolbox in the garage when I was growing up, and every drawer had a different type of wrench or screwdriver. He was obsessed with organizing it. He even traced the outline of every tool onto this foam stuff so you'd know exactly where it went back. Foam is so weird if you think about it. It's mostly just trapped air. Like memory foam mattresses. I bought one of those a few years ago, the kind that comes rolled up in a box. It takes like two whole days to fully expand, and it smells like a chemical factory for the first week. \n\nFactories are so loud. I took a tour of a chocolate factory once on a field trip in middle school. They gave us these little paper hairnets to wear, which was embarrassing, but the chocolate smelled amazing. They had this massive vat of caramel just spinning around...\n\nWait, sorry, what were you asking me? Something about coats?\n\nDoctor: Have you ever been told by a doctor that you have any thyroid problems, vitamin deficiencies, or any other metabolic conditions?\n\nPatient: Thyroid problems? Um, no, I don't think any doctor has ever told me that. I mean, I try to take vitamins sometimes, like those gummy ones? They taste just like candy, which is probably dangerous for kids. I remember my little cousin ate a whole bottle of Flintstones vitamins once because he thought they were sweet tarts. They had to call poison control and everything. Poison control operators must have such stressful jobs, just sitting by the phone waiting for someone to panic. \n\nI hate talking on the phone. It gives me so much anxiety because you can't see the other person's face. That's why I prefer texting. I text my manager at the restaurant all the time because our schedule is always changing. Last week she scheduled me for a double shift on Saturday, which is the busiest day. We had this huge party of fifteen come in right at the dinner rush, and they all wanted separate checks. Splitting checks is the absolute worst part of being a waitress. Our point-of-sale system is so old, the screen barely registers when you tap it. It's like one of those old ATMs where you have to press the buttons super hard. \n\nATMs always charge ridiculous fees now. I saw one at a gas station that wanted four dollars just to take out a twenty. Gas stations smell so weird, like a mix of gasoline and cheap hot dogs. They always have those hot dogs rolling on the little metal grates...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Was it about my work schedule?\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any difficulty in completing familiar tasks at home, such as cooking a meal or using household appliances?\n\nPatient: Familiar tasks? Um, no, I don't think so. I can still cook and everything just fine. I made spaghetti just last night. Boiling water is pretty easy, though my stove takes absolutely forever to heat up. It's one of those electric ones with the flat glass top. I really miss having a gas stove. My old apartment had a gas stove, and it cooked everything so evenly, but I was always terrified I'd leave it on and blow the place up. You know how they add that rotten egg smell to gas so you can tell if there's a leak? It's kind of like how some candles smell way too strong when you first open them. \n\nI used to work at this candle shop in the mall a few years ago, and during the holidays, the cinnamon scent would give me massive headaches. The mall is always a complete nightmare around Christmas anyway. People park like absolute maniacs. Someone actually dinged my car door last December and didn't even leave a note. Car insurance is such a scam, honestly. You pay them every single month, and then when something actually happens, they fight you on paying for it. My deductible is so high right now I couldn't even afford to get the dent fixed, so I just have to look at it every time I get in to drive to work. \n\nWait, what did you ask me about? Was it about my car?\n\nDoctor: Have you always been someone who prefers to keep to yourself, or have you often felt that people around you, like your coworkers or neighbors, are talking about you or looking at you differently?\n\nPatient: People looking at me? I mean, I guess I used to be pretty social, but lately... I don't know. I keep feeling like a couple of the servers at work and this one regular customer are always watching me. Like, they're watching exactly how I ring things into the POS system. And they leave these little signs on the tickets, like weird marks or folded corners, just to prove they're trying to get me fired. My husband keeps telling me it's just normal restaurant mess-ups, but he doesn't work there, you know? \n\nRestaurants have so much drama anyway. It's like high school all over again. I hated high school. The cafeteria food was always so gross, especially those square pizzas they used to serve. Why were they square? Pizza is supposed to be round. I guess it fits on the tray better. Trays are so annoying to carry when they're fully loaded. At work, we have these huge oval trays for carrying drinks, and if you don't balance them exactly right in the center of your palm, the whole thing just tips over. I spilled a whole tray of ice waters on a table once when I first started serving. Ice is actually really bad for your digestion, did you know that? It shocks your system. That's why in Europe they don't really put ice in their drinks. \n\nI've always wanted to go to Europe, maybe Italy or something. The architecture there is supposed to be amazing, all those old stone buildings that have been standing for hundreds of years. Stones are so heavy, I can't imagine how they moved them all without modern cranes and stuff. Construction sites are so loud now, with all the backup beepers on the trucks...\n\nWait, sorry. What were we talking about? Was it about my husband?\n\nDoctor: When you think these coworkers are watching you and leaving marks, do you feel like you can hear them talking about you when they aren't actually nearby?\n\nPatient: Hearing them? Well, it's not always them, exactly. But sometimes, yeah, like when I'm closing up the restaurant or even just at home in my own kitchen, I'll hear this woman's voice. She'll say my name or tell me I'm doing the register count wrong. It's so clear that I actually answer her out loud before I realize nobody else heard it. \n\nCounting the drawer is always so stressful anyway, especially when people pay with exact change in pennies. Who even carries pennies anymore? They're practically worthless. I heard they cost more to make than they're actually worth. The government should just get rid of them like they did in Canada. Canada seems nice, though it gets so cold up there. I hate the cold. Whenever winter comes around, my knuckles get so dry they crack and bleed, and no amount of lotion ever helps. Lotion is always so greasy, too, especially the ones with shea butter. You put it on and then you can't open doorknobs for like an hour. \n\nDoorknobs in public restrooms are the worst, though. I always use a paper towel to open them on my way out. People are so gross, they don't even wash their hands. That's how germs spread so fast, especially during flu season. The flu shot always makes my arm ache for days... \n\nWait, what did you ask me? Was it about the register?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned these experiences have been going on for over a year; have you had these feelings of being watched or hearing that voice for at least the last six months straight?\n\nPatient: Six months? Um, no, the really clear voices, and like, knowing for sure that my coworkers and those customers are targeting me... that's been going on for about four months. Four months ago was right around the time the weather started getting super hot. I absolutely hate the summer heat. Our AC at the restaurant broke down right in the middle of a Friday dinner rush, and it was like a complete sauna in there. You'd think the owners would fix it immediately, but they are so cheap. They buy the absolute cheapest napkins, too—the ones that basically dissolve if you try to wipe up a single drop of condensation. \n\nWater rings on wooden tables drive me crazy, though. My grandmother had this beautiful mahogany dining table, and she made us use coasters for absolutely everything. Coasters are kind of a dying thing, aren't they? Nobody uses them anymore. People just let their drinks ruin their furniture. Furniture is so incredibly expensive now, especially couches. I tried shopping for a new couch last weekend and everything was either gray or beige. Whatever happened to actual color? I'd love a nice deep green velvet couch. Velvet is so hard to clean, though, especially if you have pets. My friend has a cat that just sheds everywhere, and it gets all over your black pants when you sit down. Lint rollers never actually pick up all the hair, either...\n\nWait, what were we talking about? Was it about my friend's cat?\n\nDoctor: You mentioned feeling like people were watching you for the last four months, but before that, had you already been feeling like you didn't care about your hobbies or interacting with others for a few months prior?\n\nPatient: Hobbies? I guess so, yeah. Even before the stuff at work started getting really intense, I had pretty much stopped doing all the things I used to like. I used to go for walks on the beach, bake, garden, write in my journal... but I don't really do any of that much anymore. Even when my husband tries to talk with me, I mostly just sit there because it feels like I can't get myself to care or start anything. \n\nStarting things is always the hardest part, isn't it? Like when you have to start a new book, and the first few chapters are just so boring because they're setting up the world. I used to read a lot of fantasy novels, the ones with the really long, complicated maps in the front. Maps are so confusing to read, especially the ones on your phone when the GPS gets turned around and tells you to go south when you're already going north. I got so lost driving to my dentist appointment last year because of that. Dentists always make you feel so guilty if you don't floss every single day. I try to floss, but the string always gets stuck between my back teeth and shreds. The wax kind is better, but it tastes like weird fake mint. Mint is supposed to be refreshing, but I think it just makes cold water hurt your teeth even more. Water is so important to drink, though. They say you should drink eight glasses a day, but who actually keeps track of that? \n\nWait, what was the question? Was it about reading?\n\nDoctor: Looking back, did you notice that you were struggling to keep up with your normal responsibilities at work or feeling withdrawn from your friends for a significant period of time, even before you started feeling like people were watching you or hearing that voice?\n\nPatient: Yeah, the whole... the whole change in me, it's been building for a while. Probably about a year and two months now? It all started right around the time they asked me to take on the evening shift lead responsibilities at the restaurant. \n\nBeing a shift lead is actually way more annoying than it sounds. You don't even get that much of a pay bump, but suddenly everything is your fault. Like if the prep cooks don't chop enough onions for the dinner rush, everyone looks at you. Onions are so weird, right? Like, why do they make us cry? I read once it's some sort of sulfur gas they release when you cut them. Gas stoves are supposed to be better for cooking, but I'm always terrified I'm going to leave one on and blow up the house. My aunt actually had a small fire in her kitchen once because she left a dish towel too close to the burner. Dish towels get so stained anyway, especially if you use them to wipe up tomato sauce. Tomato sauce is impossible to get out of white clothes. I ruined my favorite white blouse that way. It was silk, too. Silk is so high maintenance, you always have to dry clean it. The dry cleaners near my house always smells like weird chemicals, and the lady who runs it is so grumpy. She never smiles, not even when you say good morning...\n\nWait, what did you ask me? Was it about the onions?\n\nDoctor: Looking back over the entire last year and two months, have you experienced these feelings of being watched or heard that voice at any point prior to those last four months?\n\nPatient: No, not really. Before four months ago, I didn't have the really clear voices or that absolute certainty that my coworkers and the customers were out to get me. That specific stuff, the targeting, that's only been the last four months. \n\nFour months is a third of a year, which sounds like a long time when you say it like that. Time is just so weird lately. Like, I bought a planner at the start of the year because I was determined to be more organized. It had this beautiful floral cover, like cherry blossoms. Cherry blossoms only bloom for such a short time in the spring, it's almost tragic. People travel all the way to Washington D.C. just to see them. Traveling sounds exhausting right now, though. The airports are always so crowded, and you have to take your shoes off at security. I always end up wearing socks with a hole in the toe when I go to the airport, it never fails. Socks disappear in the laundry all the time. I'm convinced washing machines eat them. My washing machine makes this horrible clanking sound on the spin cycle, like someone threw a handful of quarters in there. Quarters are basically useless now anyway, except for parking meters. But even parking meters take credit cards now... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Was it about my washing machine?"
